### Two more, taken 2026-09-06 (after the ~04:45 ruling)

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **A deleted or missing source gets its own empty state.** Operator, verbatim option: *"New 'source missing' state"* | `055` AC-005 and its own T005 had been reading the same condition two ways — T005 as `getEmptyStateReason`'s `sourceCount === 0 -> no-matching-data`, AC-005 as the hero path's `no-database` — and the row could not close while both readings stood. **Neither wins.** A view whose source folder was deleted is a third condition and renders a distinct flavour with its own copy and a **"Choose database"** action; `no-database` keeps its own meaning (a view that never named a database) and `no-matching-data` keeps its own (a source that exists and matched nothing). Red today, measured: `EmptyStateReason` holds thirteen reasons and none is the source-missing flavour, `empty-state-renderer.ts:217` maps `sourceCount === 0` onto `no-matching-data`, and no *Choose database* action exists anywhere in `src/`. **Landed 2026-09-06 by the follow-up leg**, with one correction to the ruling's own shorthand: the new reason is chosen when the configured source no longer resolves in the vault, not on `sourceCount === 0`, because that count cannot tell a deleted folder from an empty one and would have shown a brand-new database a lost-source card. The table's other two rows are untouched | `055-states-feedback-and-motion/decision-record.md` ADR-009 (**Accepted**); `055`'s T005 and AC-005, both rewritten off the contradiction onto the new flavour with its threshold and red value; `050-anytype-adoption` REQ-009, whose three-tier ladder the new flavour renders at tier 2; `src/views/empty-state-renderer.ts` when the follow-up leg lands |
| **The gantt keeps ours: the 24px container inset and the antialiased font smoothing stay plugin-wide.** Operator, verbatim option: *"Keep ours"* | Two of the five differences the 2026-09-06 pixel re-verification found between our gantt and the Project Manager reference are **host chrome, not gantt values**: `.note-database-container`'s 24px inline padding (whole-widget inset 40/48 CSS ours against the reference's 16/16, and the 8 device px every timeline x sits left of the reference's) and `styles.css:36`'s `-webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased` (13-17% fewer ink pixels throughout). Both apply to every view this plugin renders, so matching the reference on the gantt would mean changing them plugin-wide for one view's sake. The operator keeps ours. **The gantt itself already matches**: the `.pm-gantt-*` block is 50-of-51 rules declaration-identical to the vendored `gantt.css` (the 51st our own phone touch-target override, base rule identical) and the geometry matches to the pixel. Nothing to fix, and nothing left to align | §4 rows 37 and 38, whose *five visible differences* text this ruling dispositions rather than amends — the two host-chrome entries are now **decided**, not merely named; `037-timeline-gantt-port`, whose 1:1 port stands; `047-competitor-references-and-pm-alignment` AC-006 and T013/T014, whose *nothing to fix* verdict this confirms. Rows 37 and 38 stay open on their own remaining subject, the operator's side-by-side in the vault, which nothing in this repository can close |

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **Amend `044`'s frozen row-padding predicate rather than rename the properties panel's rows.** Operator, verbatim: *"Amend the predicate"* | `hasPaddedRows` accepts `.db-column-manager-row` as a fourth row synonym, so the properties panel measures under all eight grammar columns instead of the overflow-only sweep. The alternative — renaming the panel's rows onto `.db-panel-row` — is a DOM and stylesheet change on a captured surface, and the row already clears the 2px padding floor under its own pre-existing rule, so the gap was the predicate's vocabulary and never the panel's markup. No `styles.css` change and no recapture were owed. The registry goes from 13 registered surfaces to **14**, all 8/8 | `054-record-and-relation-surfaces/decision-record.md` ADR-006 (**Decided**); `src/views/sheet-grammar.ts`'s one selector and its header comment, which commits to naming each accepted synonym rather than letting the list grow silently; `tools/live/sheet-grammar.mjs`'s `REGISTERED_SURFACES`, `column-manager` moved out of the overflow-only list; `044-phone-sheet-alignment/spec.md` §3, one sentence recording the amendment against the freeze it amends; `054`'s T071; §5.A's `054` row |
| **Close the componentization census on what built the row, not on what class it carries.** Operator, verbatim: *"Source census of builder calls"* | Goal D3's observable moves from a DOM class census to a source census. `buildPropertyRow` and `buildDesktopRecordHeader` take their class names from their caller by design, so four consumers emit four class names off one builder and a DOM count reads 4 for a convergence that already happened; forcing one shared class would move captures this packet does not own. The threshold is zero hand-built headers or rows. **The check as first written did not meet it** — reading only a `cls:` string in a DOM-create call's first argument, it passed a `classList.add` bypass silently at exit 0, and would have missed the `createChild` helper the peek's own retired header and rows were actually built with; it now reads every string literal and excludes only CSS selectors and shared-builder class options, with five construction forms each seen red | `054-record-and-relation-surfaces/decision-record.md` ADR-007 (**Decided**); `goal.md` D3's amendment note; `tools/live/surface-census.mjs` §6b and its stamp's `recordSurfaceHandBuilt` total; `054`'s T011, T023 and T070; `checklist.md` C15; §5.A's `054` row |

### One more, taken 2026-09-06 (~07:50)

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **Drop the confirm for a single delete and make it reversible instead.** Operator, verbatim: *"No confirm for single delete, Undo toast"* | Closes `051` ADR-007's **E4**, the one hold that ADR flagged rather than took. The confirm stays for **bulk delete and anything not undoable**; a single delete raises an undo toast and no dialog. This is neither reading E4 offered: it reaches Anytype's no-confirm pattern without routing deletions through a Bin, by making the single delete reversible at the interaction layer rather than at the storage layer. **Implemented by `055` T019, not by the leg that recorded it** — `051` exported the confirm primitive and closed the componentization census; removing the single-delete confirm was a separate leg, and the primitive is what the surviving bulk and not-undoable confirms keep using. As built, the confirm moved out of `row-menu.ts` and into `deleteRow` in both classes, gated on `canUndoDeletion(app, file)` (`modals/confirm-modal.ts`): a readable row skips the confirm and gets the toast's Undo, an unreadable one still confirms and offers no Undo **Read from source rather than assumed**: `row-menu.ts:166-176`'s single-row delete already calls `confirmWithModal` **and** `deleteRow` (`database-view.ts:8354-8371`) already raises a `showToast` with an Undo action, so the fix removes one confirm call rather than building the toast; `database-view.ts:4962`'s bulk `deleteSelectedRows` is unaffected by name, per the ruling's own carve-out | `051-modal-and-sheet-componentization/decision-record.md` ADR-007 E4 (**Closed**); `055-states-feedback-and-motion/decision-record.md` ADR-010 (**Accepted — built**), `goal.md`, `acceptance-criteria.md` AC-013 (**Met**) and `tasks.md` T019 — `055` owns the `deleteRow` call-site change; §6A here |

**What the second one is an instance of.** A census is a claim about what a regex or a walker can
see, and its threshold is only as strong as the forms it recognises. Both of these rulings closed
rows that had been open for legs — and the second one closed on a check that, as delivered, could
not have gone red on the very code whose retirement it was certifying. The zero was true; the
check that read it was not yet able to read a different zero. **A census lands with its bypass
controls written in the forms the codebase actually uses, or it lands as decoration.**

What shipped took only the second half: the chip stayed `background: transparent` and only the text darkened, which clears 4.5:1 (amber `#915608` on white, 5.93:1) but is not the pattern the ADR named and reads as a visible difference against the reference. The ruling restores the fill | `056-board-anytype-parity/decision-record.md` ADR-006 (**Implemented** 2026-09-06); `056/tasks.md` T012 R6, closed. `.db-kanban-col-chip` now reads `background: var(--db-status-bg, transparent)`, the same fill property the card's own tag chip already used, moving 44 board captures under the CSS lane |
| **The board's grey option pair is neutral.** Operator, verbatim: *"Neutral, match Anytype"* | `design-trueup.md` §3 A9 measured Anytype's grey pair as neutral — tint `#E3E3E3`, text `#888888` light and `#A8A8A8` dark. It is not the tightest bucket: an independent re-measure at the landing put light teal `#1B7471` on `#CFEEED` at **4.52:1**, and the grey row second. **Both rulings arrived while the residual leg was open and were not folded into it; a follow-up leg
landed both together.** The instruction that opened `056`'s R-rows also said to leave R6 and R7
alone, so the leg that fixed the other eight recorded these two and changed nothing — which is why
`056` carried AC-011 as **Met** with two rows still open under it, the criterion being about measured
values reaching the surface rather than about colour decisions the operator had not yet taken. A
follow-up leg then landed both in one stylesheet edit: all ten option-colour tint/text pairs plus the
ungrouped fallback re-verified at WCAG 1.4.3's 4.5:1, text-on-tint and text-on-page, both themes —
every row clears it. `056/tasks.md` T012 and `acceptance-criteria.md` AC-011 now carry all ten
residuals fixed, none open.

**Re-measured independently at the landing, and the tints checked against the source rather than
assumed.** Twenty-two pairs (ten option buckets plus the ungrouped fallback, both themes), all at or
above 4.5:1 on the tint **and** on the page, no exception to name: tightest light teal `#1B7471` on
`#CFEEED` at **4.52:1**, widest light blue `#0B35DA` on `#DDE3FB` at **6.49:1**. Every one of the ten
tints is byte-for-byte the value `design-trueup.md` §3 A9 measured off Anytype's own column menu —
none was altered to buy contrast — so only the text moved, which is what R6 asked for. The R1/R2
geometry landed earlier survives the fill: the header chip's painted band still reads **48 device px
(24 CSS)** and the card's property rhythm **25 CSS px** off the recaptured light desktop board.

All of those numbers still hold. What none of them establishes is whether the surface, read whole, is the reference's — and that is what the operator just judged. **A value-level Met cannot answer a gestalt question, and where the two disagree the operator's reading wins** (D3: only the operator's confirmation closes). **No measured row is un-ticked**: withdrawing a true measurement to express a different judgement would destroy the record and answer nothing. A new criterion opens instead, and **the threshold it was left waiting for has now landed**: it is every one of `acceptance-criteria.md`'s **G1-G15** rows Met, each re-measured per pixel on a corpus recaptured on HEAD and on a second-theme capture (G12), with **0 of 15** green today | `057`'s new `goal.md` OPERATOR/GESTALT criterion; `acceptance-criteria.md` **G1-G15**; `tasks.md` **T019** (the rebuild) and **T020** (the stagger folded into it); `review-ui-calendar-2026-09-06.md`; `decision-record.md`'s 2026-09-06 note, which carries the review's P0-2 Monday-start default as **Proposed** rather than taken; `roadmap.md` §4 row 63, §5.A and §7.14 |

Red is the operator's own catalogue on the desktop profile with the switch off: rows at 36/130/300px, the tallest set by a long-text Journal column carrying `wrap: true` written by the fixture emitter rather than by anyone using the product — so the one control named *Wrap text* could not flatten the one column that was not flat. Green is the 3x2 matrix: off clips Wrap, Follow and Clip alike; on wraps Wrap and Follow and leaves Clip clipped. A column mode becomes a refinement of a wrapping table rather than an exemption from a clipped one, and the cost is named — a per-column *always wrap this one* is no longer expressible. **The earlier markdown-`<br>` diagnosis is not withdrawn, it is demoted**: it is a real, narrower producer, kept and tested, but the column the operator photographed is plain text and the markdown column in that catalogue holds one-line values | `053-toolbar-and-view-controls/decision-record.md` **ADR-004, amended in place and dated** (the Decision block marked superseded-in-part); `src/data/column-types.ts`'s `resolvesToWrappedCell`, the single resolver `cell-renderer.ts` and `column-width.ts` both read; `cell-renderer-wrap.test.ts`'s matrix and `column-width.test.ts`'s three auto-fit cases; `tools/live/render-assertions.mjs`'s desktop-profile wrap pass; `roadmap.md` §4 row 60 |
| **The switch is honoured on the phone.** Operator, verbatim: *"Honour the switch on phone"* | Wrap on wraps text on a phone; wrap off clips at the 36px row floor. Red is a specificity defect nobody had measured: `.is-phone …​ .db-table td:not(.db-select-col):not(.db-record-icon-col)` set `white-space: nowrap` at five classes against `td.db-cell-wrap`'s three, so the switch had **never** wrapped text on a phone. It looked wired only because the value containers are flex boxes that wrap on their own — turning it on stacked the chips and left every sentence on one line, and a row-height assertion passed on that. Two questions had been answered by one declaration: how much width a table may spend on a narrow screen, which is still the phone rule's, and whether a cell may take a second line, which is the switch's | `styles.css`'s phone table-cell rules (the wrapping cell excluded from the `nowrap` declaration, its 60vw width cap kept in a rule of its own); `tools/live/render-assertions.mjs`'s text-wrap assertion on the phone pass, which fails on a row that grew from chips alone; the two `table-wrap-on-mobile-*` captures, the only ones in the sweep whose content moved | 

### Three more, taken 2026-09-06 (~15:50 to ~16:25)

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **The sheet family's deep-research loop runs now, on the current state.** Operator, verbatim: *"Run it now on the current state."* | `051`'s own criterion had the loop **starting** only once `044`, `048` and `051` were each done **and verified as planned** — the operator's earlier words, *"once done and verified"*. That gate is lifted for this run and only for this run: the loop reads the tree as it stands, defects and all, rather than a finished family. What is **not** lifted is the loop's shape — **10 iterations**, `--stop-policy=max-iterations`, GLM 5.3 flash max, bounded prompts with an explicit file list and no image reads, a fresh worktree, an Opus synthesis afterwards. Running early costs precision, not method: findings against a tree that is still moving are a claim about a moving target, so the synthesis re-reads HEAD before it writes a phase document. Dispatched ~15:50 in `.worktrees/172-research-sheet-family` on DevPass | `051-modal-and-sheet-componentization/goal.md`'s deep-research criterion and its **Starts when** row, amended in place and dated; `051/tasks.md` T025; `roadmap.md` §4 row 59 |
| **Notion refinement is added to every UI phase, five research iterations per surface.** Operator, verbatim: *"Based on notion screenshots add phases to all ui improvement phases to further refine based on notion ui screenshots. But do 5 iters of deep research with glm 5.3 flash max on those screens per relevant phase."* | Eight surfaces, eight reserved children `059`-`066`, one pipeline each: a **Sonnet digest** of the relevant Notion captures at `<phase>/notion-screens-digest.md`, then `/deep:research:auto` at **5 iterations** under `--stop-policy=max-iterations` on **GLM 5.3 flash max**, then an **Opus synthesis** that opens the child. The digest exists for one reason and it is worth stating: **GLM cannot read images**, so a capture reaches the loop as measured prose or not at all. Wave 1 — `059` board, `060` calendar, `061` sheets, `062` table — has been running since **16:14** in `worktrees/174`-`177`; wave 2 — `063` dropdowns, `064` toolbar, `065` record, `066` states — is queued. Each lands through a fresh Opus verifier (D4). **The refinement is additive**: a child may add a criterion, a task or an ADR, and may not un-tick a measured row or overturn a landed Anytype ruling — where a Notion finding contradicts one, it becomes a **Proposed** ADR naming both readings. §7.15 carries the conflict | `goal.md` **D15** and its reserved-children table; `roadmap.md` §5.A's `059`-`066` block, §7.15; a dated amendment in each of `051`, `052`, `053`, `054`, `055`, `056`, `057` and `058`'s own `goal.md` |
| **The GLM route is OpenRouter until its credit is gone, then DevPass.** Operator, verbatim: *"use openrouter untill usage is 0 then devpass."* | It settles transport, not model: `openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` at max effort is tried first and `llmgateway` (DevPass) is the fallback, so a run never stalls on an empty balance and never silently upgrades to a different model. OpenRouter credit read **$1.65 of $30 at 16:25**, which is why the fallback is wired rather than documented — the pipeline attempts OpenRouter and falls back per run. Model ids are passed exactly as each transport spells them, never a near-miss (D14) | `051/goal.md`'s deep-research **Executors** row; the `059`-`066` pipeline in §5.A and in each phase's amendment; `.opencode/skills/cli-external-orchestration/cli-pi/SKILL.md` stays the contract to read before composing any prompt |

|---|---|---|
| **The sheet family's deep-research loop runs now, on the tree as it stands.** Operator, verbatim: *"Run it now on the current state"* | The 10:04 standing instruction attached a precondition to itself: the loop starts *"once done and verified as planned"*, which under parent D3 means the operator's device read on all three of `044`, `048` and `051`. That precondition was **measured NOT MET** at `main` `5aeb7087` and the measurement is in `051/tasks.md` T025 — three open device rows, `048`'s T025, and `051`'s own T010 / T015 / T023. The measured verdicts are left standing in `051/goal.md` and `051/tasks.md` rather than deleted — a precondition erased once it is overridden leaves no record that anything was overridden | `051/tasks.md` **T025** (ticked, with the run record and three runtime quirks); `051/goal.md`'s deep-research criterion (ticked) and its 2026-09-06 amendment; `051/acceptance-criteria.md` **AC-014** (now `Met`); the new packet **`067-sheet-family-remediation`**; dated amendments in `044/goal.md` and `048/goal.md`; `roadmap.md` §4 row 59 and §5.A |

### Three more, taken 2026-09-06 (~18:07), and they rename the product
| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **The plugin is renamed to Obnotion, and the scope is everything.** Operator, verbatim: *"our plugin is still named note database but rename to obnotion"* — asked where it had to show, *"In community plugin"*; asked how far it reached, *"Everything, including CSS class prefixes"* | Three sentences that together settle three different questions. The first names the product. The second puts `manifest.json`'s **`id`** in scope, because the community plugin panel reads `id` and `name` out of that file and Obsidian uses the id as the **directory** under `.obsidian/plugins/` — so `data.json`, the `obsidian://show-plugin?id=` deep link and the release runbook's iCloud target all move with it. The third puts the internal class vocabulary in scope: **17,099** `db-` occurrences in **1,724** distinct tokens, **1,201** of them `.db-*` selectors and **209** `--db-*` custom properties, plus **3,181** `note-database-container` and **3,375** `note-database` references in `styles.css`+`src` alone. What the ruling does **not** reach is stated so it cannot drift back in: the GitHub repository name, the root `README.md` rewrite (a parallel leg's), `specs/` history, and `db_view` — the frontmatter key in the user's own notes | the new packet **`068-rename-to-obnotion`**, its `goal.md` D1-D8 and its six ADRs; `manifest.json`, `package.json`, `src/main.ts`, `src/i18n.ts`, `styles.css`, `tools/**`, `.storybook/`, `screenshots/`; `goal.md`'s phase-subgoal table; `roadmap.md` §4 row 64 and §7.16 |
| **An existing install must survive the rename with no manual step.** Corollary of the id change, taken with it rather than after it | The old `data.json` is **copied, never moved** on first load under the new id, and the source is never renamed or deleted, so a revert costs nothing and a user running both plugins loses nothing (`068` ADR-002). Separately, **five strings keep permanent aliases** with no deprecation window (`068` ADR-004): the code-block languages `note-database` and `database-view` that users typed into their own notes, the view types `note-database-view` and `note-database-file-view` that Obsidian persists in the vault's `workspace.json`, and the export marker `note-database-csv-markdown`. A hard rename of any of the five breaks content that already exists in somebody else's files, which is not a trade this program gets to make | `068/acceptance-criteria.md` **AC-005** and **AC-006**, which enumerate the five surviving strings so REQ-002's "zero occurrences" has a named expected answer rather than a fuzzy one; `068/tasks.md` T010 and T011 |
| **It runs as one leg, last, and the four-phase recommendation is declined on the record.** `recommend-level.sh --loc 4314 --files 134 --api --db --architectural` returned **90/100, Level 3, phase score 50/50, suggested_phase_count 4** | The level is adopted; the phase count is not. Both `phase-definitions.md` §2 thresholds are met, so the tool is right about the scale — and wrong about the shape, because the whole risk of this change is that it moves **every class name at once**. Four phases would mean four rebase windows and four recaptures across a tree whose class vocabulary is in motion, which is precisely the collision this program has already paid for twice (`port-phase-landing-discipline`'s `styles.css` collisions, and `rerere` silently reverting upstream content on a replayed rebase). So: one leg, one rebase window, sequenced **after 0.0.30 and after `058`, `056`'s edge-reveal, `057`'s month-chip and every `059`-`067` child**, holding the CSS lane end to end. **Two rows stay the operator's**: ADR-003's `obn-` prefix, which is **Proposed** until they accept it, and `manifest.json`'s `author`/`authorUrl`/`fundingUrl`, which still credit pangy9 from the fork point — the recommendation there is to attribute the fork to MichelKerkmeester and keep upstream credit in the README | `068` **ADR-003** (Proposed) and **ADR-006** (Accepted); `068/tasks.md` T001 and T004; `tools/lane/css-lane.json`, held by `068` for the leg and re-pinned on landing; `068/spec.md` §12 Q1-Q4 |

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **The table cell action menu is redesigned to Notion's grammar.** Operator, verbatim: *"This menu still has extremely bad ui ux, the attached menu you see when you click a cell, should be redesigned to mimic how notion would do it."* Two iOS captures came with it | Under **D15** Notion is additive and Anytype parity is the default. This ruling names Notion as the reference for **this one surface**, and it does not widen: `050`'s measured Anytype values stay the values of record everywhere else, and the eight Notion-versus-Anytype conflicts the sheets loop found remain recorded with **zero** rulings overridden. No Notion capture in the 3,647-file harvest shows a multi-cell selection in a table at all, so the single-row bar is **our** shape at Notion's density and is labelled that way rather than claimed as parity | `061-notion-sheet-refinement` **REQ-001**, **AC-001** to **AC-003**, **ADR-002**, **ADR-003**, **ADR-004**; `roadmap.md` §4 row 65 and §5.A; the device questions appended to `067` **AC-011** |

### Two more, taken 2026-09-06 (~19:00), and the first widens the evidence rather than answering the question
| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **The cell surface is decided from four products, not from Notion alone.** Put the five `Proposed` ADRs and asked to rule, the operator declined the evidence and widened it, verbatim: *"Check anytype, evernote, fibery and find best ui ux approach for this"* | This is a ruling about **what counts as evidence**, and it changed the answer rather than confirming it. **50 captures** were read directly — 14 Notion (iOS and web), 12 Anytype (phone and desktop), 12 Evernote (iOS and web), 12 Fibery (desktop; the harvest has no phone platform), each path checked against disk — and the finding is unanimous: **zero of four products dock a labelled action bar to the frame's bottom edge.** So the operator's first capture is not a bar that wraps, it is a bar that should not exist on a phone. The model that lands instead: a tap **edits** and paints no selection; selection is entered by a **long press**; its whole chrome is a **three-control anchored pill** — live count, one `Copy`, `···` — 44px high, anchored 8px above the range and clamped clear of Obsidian's navigation through the same `--db-mobile-navbar-height` the FAB already reads; everything past `Copy` is a **titled bottom sheet** of labelled rows in three groups, `Clear` last and the only red one; the value editor is drawn **at the cell**; desktop keeps its 30px bar collapsed to five children with an anchored `···` menu. **This supersedes the single-row-bar half of the ~17:07 ruling above** — that ruling named Notion as the reference for this surface and still does; only the shape derived from it changed, because the wider read removed its premise. **Landed rulings overridden: zero.** Anytype has no cell selection in twelve read captures, so the model fills a silence rather than contradicting `051` ADR-007's parity default; the one dissent, Anytype phone pushing a grid cell's editor to a sheet, is registered as **C-I** rather than silently outvoted | `061-notion-sheet-refinement` **ADR-000** (the read itself, with all 50 citations as a path manifest, and the four-app comparison), **ADR-002**, **ADR-003**, **ADR-004** — all now **Accepted**; **AC-002**, **AC-003** re-cut and **AC-008** added for the desktop half; **REQ-001** rewritten; `tasks.md` **T001** closed and **T005** to **T007** re-cut, with **T011** added for the `···` sheet and the desktop collapse; `roadmap.md` §4 row 65 and §5.A |
| **The destructive confirm is a centred card with stacked buttons.** Operator, verbatim: *"Yes, centred card with stacked buttons"* | Taken in the same sitting, and it is the one row here that **accepts a proposal unchanged**. The confirm keeps its sheet mount — `super(app, "sheet")` stays, because the shell's `dialog` presentation stays a centred dialog everywhere and a phone modal must become a stacked sheet under `048` **D1** — and gains a **declared** third frame role beside the existing floating/flush split: inset ≥ 16px on all four edges, `--db-radius-xl` on all four corners, actions stacked full width at ≥ 44px. The stacked row is scoped to the confirm on **both** platforms; the 16px card inset is phone-only. Notion fills a gap here rather than contradicting a ruling — `051/design-trueup.md` row 1 reads "Not seen" for Anytype across 118 iOS states and 600 menus | `061` **ADR-001** (**Accepted**), **REQ-002**, **AC-004**; `tasks.md` **T009**; `tools/live/sheet-grammar.mjs`'s confirm row. **AC-005 stays the operator's** — it is read on a device in the same sitting as `067` **AC-011** and nothing in this repository can close it |

### One more, taken 2026-09-06 (~19:08), on the option colour picker

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **The option colour picker becomes a one-column labelled list.** Operator, verbatim: *"Check fibery, anytype and notion and suggest best ui ux"* | Three products were read capture by capture and they agree on a rule none of them writes down: **the shape follows the persistence of the colour.** A colour that names a persistent attribute gets a labelled row — Anytype's only colour picker is eleven named rows, 28px pitch, 16px dot, 224px wide, trailing tick (`screenshots/anytype/desktop/menus/anytype-menu-object-block-menu-color-dark.png`, and `052`'s G15 measured the same panel), and Notion's option colours are ten named rows on **both** platforms (web `notion-web-flow-editing-an-option-03-61c3069b`, ~28-30px row and ~20-22px swatch; iOS `notion-ios-flow-adding-a-conditional-color-07-7e1fda1e`, a full-height `Select color` sheet at 44.8pt). A colour that is transient inline formatting gets an unlabelled grid — Notion's text/background picker (`notion-web-editors-page-23-e9553f06`) and Fibery's 2x10 circle grid, which colours a **view rule** (`worktrees/150-harvest-fibery` `3b3ac633`, `fibery-web-flow-editing-chart-color-02-c64ab346`). Fibery is the control: it has a swatch grid **and** persistent options, and gives its options no colour at all (`fibery-web-flow-reordering-field-options-03-a7c1f3a8`). Ours is the persistent case and ships the transient shape. Three stated facts were also wrong and are corrected on the record: the palette is **sixteen** colours, not twelve (`status-colors.ts:13-16`); the rendered panel is **96px**, not the 124 its width role declares (`styles.css:7277-7294` over `popover-host.ts:236-240`); and sixteen hues cannot be read as sixteen choices — measured in CIE76 ΔE over the shipped fills, light theme puts `gray`/`slate` **2.8** apart, dark theme puts five pairs under **10**. That last number is the ground: `051` ADR-007 **E3**'s own WCAG 1.4.1 rule makes the visible name the missing non-colour signal, so this is not a preference | `063-notion-dropdown-refinement` ADR-004, amended from Proposed to **Accepted** the same night, with REQ-008, AC-012 to AC-016 and T014 to T017. It **withdraws** that ADR's earlier "keep the grid" recommendation rather than editing it quietly. It also declines one parity value on the record: Anytype's 28px row loses to the family's own 30px (`styles.css:3245`), because 28 would move every dropdown in the family and every capture of one for 2px. And it names a capture defect that predates it — `field-option-color-picker-mobile-light.png` is shape-identical to its desktop twin, so `048`'s registered phone pair has never photographed the phone sheet |

### Four more, taken 2026-09-06 (~18:32), on the table

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **Conditional row colour gets its own view-settings row.** Operator, verbatim: *"Yes, own row in view settings"* | It settles presentation, not capability — and the reason it is here at all is that the digest got the capability wrong. Its divergence table read conditional row colour as having **no match**, and its Anytype cross-read escalated that to "a disagreement in existence". Both are wrong: `applyConditionalFormat` sets `--db-conditional-format-bg` on the `tr` (`src/data/conditional-formatting.ts:168-206`), it is wired at `src/views/table-renderer.ts:85`, `:866` and `:911`, and `tr.db-conditional-format > td` paints it (`styles.css:1317-1319`). The digest's grep pattern could not match the symbol names in use, which is `062` `goal.md` D2's rule in one line: **an absence grep is a claim, not evidence.** What the ruling moves is where the control is found — a first-class *Conditional color* row with an explainer, as Notion presents it (`142cef4e`), instead of database settings. That is a `053` toolbar item, so it lands in **`064`**, which did not exist on `main` when the ruling was taken. **It does now:** `064` opened 2026-09-07 and carries the ruling as its own criterion 8 / REQ-009 / AC-012 / T014, recorded in `064` ADR-010 — which also corrects one inherited sentence against the tree, since ours does not live in database settings: `renderConditionalFormatting` is already mounted in the view half of the panel (`view-config-panel-renderer.ts:405`) and the rules are already per-view, so the work is the named summary row and its explainer, not a relocation | `062` **ADR-003** (Accepted), which opens **no** criterion and **no** task in `062` and states what `064` inherits so nothing is re-derived |
| **The frozen column's divider is a shadow that only appears once content passes under it.** Operator, verbatim: *"Subtle shadow when scrolled past"* | A taste question answered as a threshold. At `scrollLeft === 0` the frozen boundary is indistinguishable from any other column boundary — no shadow, no extra line, no width change; once the table scrolls sideways, the last frozen column paints a soft right-edge shadow so the reader sees content passing **under** it rather than beside it. The colour is token-derived and measured in both themes under `062` ADR-004, because **no Notion capture shows a frozen state at all** (digest P7) — there was nothing to sample even if sampling were allowed. Freeze stays **desktop-only**: the phone stylesheet switches the table to `width: auto !important` with cells capped at 60vw (`styles.css:21021-21035`), so a phone table has no horizontal overflow for a frozen column to hold against, and giving it one reopens a landed phone decision | `062` **ADR-005** (Accepted, no longer split), **AC-001**; `tasks.md` T010-T013, which can land with the offsets working and the shadow added as one rule |
| **The add-row noun is a per-view configured string, falling back to today's "New".** Operator, verbatim: *"Per-view configured noun, fallback 'New'"* | Notion names what it adds and the noun varies with the source — `+ New page` (`19745d87`), `+ New task` (`e33466b4`); ours is the fixed `` `+ ${t("toolbar.new")}` `` (`src/views/table-renderer.ts:982`). Of four candidate sources the operator took the third with the fourth as fallback, which eliminates the failure the derived options carried: nothing is derived, so nothing can produce `+ New All` or `+ New Board`. Three consequences ride with the shape rather than sitting beside it — the noun is **view-scoped** and belongs to `ViewConfig`; it is **reader-authored text** and so is not a translation key, while the fallback and the surrounding `+ New` framing are and must exist in all three locales; and an **empty or whitespace-only** noun is the unconfigured case, not a button reading `+ New ` with a trailing space | `062` **ADR-006** (Accepted), **AC-008** (unblocked); `tasks.md` T025 |
| **All eight missing Notion property types ship as real types, not as labelled placeholders.** Operator, verbatim: *"All types or add more as needed"* | The research proposed shipping icons and labels first and deferring the data types. The ruling takes the largest of the three options and widens it: the rows ship **enabled, with a real data type behind each**, and the set is not capped at the four the research named. Reading `af7a18b0`'s canonical list against our union, the types we lack are exactly eight — **Person, URL, Email, Phone, created time, created by, last edited time, last edited by** — because Notion's Formula and Rollup are already ours as `computed` and `rollup`, Files & media is `files`, and Multi-select, Status, Date and Checkbox are all present. So the type count moves **13 → 21**, not the 13 → 18 the research estimated, and that correction is the operative half of the ruling. The four audit types are read-only and computed from the note, which makes them the cheap half: `row.computed` already carries values of that shape. **One implementation decision is recorded open rather than allowed to block the row**: an Obsidian vault has no user directory, so a **Person** value is either a wikilink to a person note or plain text — different storage, different rendering, different editor. That choice is owed an ADR **written before the Person renderer**, and the other seven types do not wait on it | `062` **ADR-007** (Accepted — option 1, widened), **AC-004** (unblocked, and re-cut to 21); `tasks.md` T021 across all four registries and **T021a**, the Person value-source ADR. Watch `src/views/column-menu.ts:262-264`: the submenu slices `PROPERTY_TYPES` at 6 and 9, so eight appended types land in Advanced unless the boundaries move with them |

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **Group management is adopted as one Groups panel, not as Notion's screen.** Operator, verbatim: *"Yes, one Groups panel"* | The answer settles the shape as well as the yes: **one** panel, not a panel per concern. Per-group visibility, hide-all, show-all, drag-handle reorder and "Remove grouping" all live on it rather than scattering across the column menu. It takes the `panel` role — 292-360px, local anchor, trapped focus, Escape or outside click (`design-system.md:77`, `:126`) — entered from one new row in the column menu the reader already opened, and **not** a full screen: Notion's is a screen because Notion's mobile grammar is screens, and `059` `goal.md` D2 forbids importing a grammar along with a pattern. The phone presentation goes through `044`'s sheet grammar. What made this urgent is worse than the research inferred: `hideGroup?` and `deleteGroup?` are declared at `board-renderer.ts:84-85` and **implemented by neither host** (`database-view.ts:791-830`, `embedded-database-renderer.ts:532-563`), so the two rows guarded on them at `:558-559` have never rendered, and `config.boardHiddenGroups` has no board-mounted writer or restorer at all — a visibility axis in the data model and no visibility in the UI | `059` **ADR-004** (Accepted; it was the gate on every code leg in the packet), **AC-001** to **AC-004**; `tasks.md` T006-T008, T010-T012 |

The ADR's title was changed with the ruling so the record does not read as the opposite of what was decided, and the overturned argument is **kept** rather than deleted, because it names the work the ruling creates: our board renders a shared empty card for an empty column (`board-renderer.ts:324-327`), a state committed to **eight captures** and pinned by `056` AC-011. Defaulting on means a default-configured board no longer shows those columns, so two things follow as part of the decision — the eight captures need a **named configuration** (`hideEmptyGroups: false` set explicitly, rather than relying on a default that has now moved), and the empty-column card is **not deleted**; it stays a designed state and the ruling changes only which configuration reaches it | `059` **ADR-010** (Accepted, title amended), **AC-005**; `tasks.md` T009. One flag on `ViewConfig` (`src/data/types.ts:560`), one entry in the persisted allowlist (`src/data/data-source.ts:1352`), one filter beside the hidden-group filter at `:192-193`, and the capture-fixture pin |
| **`hideGroup` is wired and `deleteGroup` is deleted.** Operator, verbatim: *"Wire hide, delete the delete action"* | One branch of this was destructive, which is why it was the operator's, and the branch taken removes the destructive affordance rather than shipping it. `hideGroup` becomes the Groups panel's toggle-off; `deleteGroup` goes, with its guard at `board-renderer.ts:558-559` and its `src/i18n.ts:136-137` "Delete group" key in **all three locales**. The third state — a declaration with no implementation — was never an option in any branch: it is why the column menu ships three rows instead of five and why two i18n keys shipped in three locales for rows nothing renders. Deleting a dead destructive action is cheaper to reverse than shipping a live one, and nothing in the record asks for group deletion from a board whose records are notes | `059` **ADR-011** (Accepted, branch two of three), **AC-001**; `tasks.md` T006 and T010. The end state is one implementation per declaration in **both** hosts — a cross-consumer property a test locks, so it cannot silently return |

### Two more, taken 2026-09-06 (~18:50), on states and feedback

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |

### Four more, taken 2026-09-06 (~19:05), and one of them widened the question it was asked

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **The hidden-properties group takes Notion's whole row grammar — and the ruling commissions a sweep for whatever else is missing.** Operator, verbatim: *"Mimic notion also regarding other features we might be missing"* | The question put was narrow: a per-row eye and a bulk link. The answer took **P3 entire** — every row carrying a drag handle, type icon, name, eye toggle and chevron; the group splitting into **Shown** and **Hidden** sections with a bulk link each; the Hidden section rendering **only when non-empty**; the count staying on the entry row exactly as `054` A4 already ruled; the title row's eye disabled because the title cannot be hidden. Two things already in the tree make it cheap: `buildCheckboxPropertyRow` (`src/views/record-surface/property-row.ts:330-395`) already carries the anatomy, and the column manager already owns the visibility persist path. **The second clause is the larger half.** It asked for a sweep of everything else Notion's record surface carries that ours does not, and the sweep's result is **one gap, not seven** — reported in full because the negative findings are the useful part: three candidates that read as gaps in the digest's prose dissolve against the code (we merge Notion's two Properties destinations onto **one** row carrying *more* affordances than Notion's chevron-only rows; our property delete already pairs a confirm with an undo entry where Notion has a trash tier; the AI-autofill chips were excluded by `054` D6 before any of this), three more are real and owned elsewhere (the comments zone by `023`/`051`, the display-mode *setting* as against its vocabulary by `006`, the page "···" menu by `051`/`052`), one rides with the deferral below, and exactly one is ours to build: **the property-visibility list cannot be searched while Notion's can** | `065` **ADR-005** (Accepted and widened) and the sweep section at the end of `065/decision-record.md`, S1-S8; **REQ-010**, **C9**, **AC-014**, `tasks.md` **T013** for the grammar; **REQ-011**, **C10**, **AC-015**, `tasks.md` **T014** for the search field. `HiddenPropertiesGroupHandle.render`'s signature changes, so the table peek moves in the same commit |
| **The record sheet's hidden group holds view-hidden columns, not empty fields.** Operator, verbatim: *"View-hidden columns, like Notion and the peek"* | One label, `panel.hiddenProperties` (`src/i18n.ts:569`), names two different populations today: the record sheet's group holds **empty fields** (`src/views/record-detail-panel.ts:384-393`) and the table peek's holds **view-hidden columns** (`src/views/table-record-peek.ts:246-248`). The user-visible consequence is that hiding a column in view config makes it vanish from the record sheet entirely while an unrelated "Hidden properties (3)" sits there counting something else. The ruling makes the peek's reading the right one, which is also Notion's (`cc8b241a`, `7ffa073f`). **Ruling order is load-bearing**: this lands before the row grammar above, because an eye that toggles *view* visibility inside a group of *empty fields* toggles nothing anybody asked for. It also creates the one question the sitting left open — where the sheet's empty-fields reveal lives once the group stops holding them — recorded as owed an ADR rather than answered by inference | `065` **ADR-006** (Accepted), **REQ-009**, **C8**, **AC-013**; `tasks.md` **T012**, sequenced before T013 and before T007 |
| **The record-level cover and icon question is Deferred.** Operator ruling, 2026-09-06 19:05 | Notion's cover system is large — a six-tab picker (`18d621d0`), a separate Reposition drag mode (`bfddb457`), an ALT badge opening an alt-text dialog (`9d90280d` → `3ebe4893`), an upload-in-progress state (`b5352c94`), hover ghost buttons on an unset header (`56e2ae1a`) — and we have no cover on any record file. **Deferred is not the same as unowned, and recording the difference is the point**: `054` D8 had it as `051`'s shell-ownership question before it is a content question, and the true-up's A1 names no cover, so there is no Anytype-parity evidence either way. The sweep's **S2** rides with this deferral rather than becoming its own row: the third of Notion's three hover ghost buttons is **"+ Add description"**, which is neither cover nor icon and which no other ADR reaches — the strip appears together or not at all | `065` **ADR-007** (**Deferred**), sweep **S2**; `tasks.md` Leg D, which now holds two unscheduled items rather than four |
| **The record sheet's add-property entry is Notion's trailing row.** Operator, verbatim: *"Trailing '+ Add a property' row"* | A straight two-way — Notion's plain-text row under the last property (`16ddd22c`, `bf2171ff`) against Anytype's `+` on the section header (`054/design-trueup.md` §A4) — answered in favour of the first, which is also the one the packet recommended and the only one the surface can actually take: **the record sheet has no section header** for Anytype's `+` to sit on. It reuses the hidden-group toggle's muted-text row language (`styles.css:10319-10346`), so it costs no new visual vocabulary. One thing is deliberately left unsettled and named as not a block: whether the row sits above or below the hidden group once the population ruling above lands. The threshold fixes it **above**, matching Notion's own order, and a later ruling moves one line | `065` **ADR-008** (Accepted), **REQ-006** ungated, **C6**, **AC-009** unblocked; `tasks.md` **T007**, whose `[B]` is lifted and whose only remaining precondition is `052`'s picker host |

### One more, taken 2026-09-06 (~19:08), and it declines a carve-out rather than granting one

| Decision | What it settles | Where it binds |
|---|---|---|
| **E3 takes no carve-out for structure-removing rows.** Operator, verbatim: *"Keep red plus icon everywhere"* | Read the status carefully: what is **Declined** is the carve-out, not E3. Notion is inconsistent about destructive rows and the inconsistency looks deliberate — red with an icon for content-destroying rows, red without an icon on `Remove` rows (`5f81b365`, `ca4fd83f`), and **plain text, no red at all** for rows that remove structure without destroying content: `Remove grouping` (`e9698e1b`), `Delete filter` (`299e69bb`). The research loop proposed a carve-out on that reading in iteration 1 and withdrew it in iteration 4 against the authority documents. The ruling closes it: `051` ADR-007 **E3** stands whole and unamended, red plus a trash icon on **every** destructive row. Notion's split is real and legible; it loses because E3's ground is **WCAG 1.4.1**, not visual weight, and two of Notion's three dispositions carry no non-colour signal at all — which is the failure E3 exists to prevent. The accepted cost is named rather than hidden: a `Remove grouping` row reads as gravely as deleting an option. A future case would still need a boundary a reviewer can apply without asking, and it would belong to `051`'s record | `063` **ADR-005** (**Declined**), `spec.md` §12's open row closed, `goal.md`'s open question closed. **Zero code changes**, and no criterion or task moves. It leaves `063` with no open ADR: ADR-004 was Accepted at ~19:08 the same night |
