## 7. WHERE A PHASE'S OWN DOCUMENTS DISAGREE

Reported, not resolved. Each names what would settle it.

### 7.1 `004` — three sources, resolved

| Source | Says |
|---|---|
| Program `goal.md` | "171/171 own appearance, **0 borrow it** (was 10); all five families have fixtures" |
| `004/acceptance-criteria.md` | **AC-001 through AC-013 all "Unmet"**, evidence cells blank |
| Lane entry 63 (08:05 today) | "Unguarded ancestor-keyed appearance rules 1 → 0"; toggles losing a property outside the container "10/10 → 0/10"; the switch taken from 34×18 to 34×28 |
| Verifier (reported to this pass) | **FAIL** — 10 toggles still ancestor-owned; a hit target at 37×24 against a 28px floor; the board, gallery, table and list fixtures contain **zero checkboxes**, so those families are measured by nothing |

**Settled.** The gate was run from a quiet tree — `npm run gate` 16 green exit 0,
`verify-placement` exit 0, the two checkbox test files 13 passed exit 0 — and the four sources
resolve into two facts and one misreading.

**The lane entry is corroborated on both its numbers.** `appearanceOwnedByAncestor: 0` of 211
controls across 57 fixtures, and the switch reaches 34×28.

**The verifier is refuted on all three of its claims and is stale.** Zero toggles are
ancestor-owned, not ten. The reach is 34×28, not 37×24. And the fixtures are not empty: board-view
28 checkboxes, board-mobile 28, gallery-view 4, list-view 24, list-mobile 12, table-view 25,
table-mobile 13, chrome-table-footer 25. The fixture finding was the more serious half precisely
because a family with no checkbox is measured by nothing — and it is the half that turned out not
to be true.

**The "all Unmet" reading is not a contradiction.** Every "Measured today" cell in that document is
dated 2026-08-29 and describes the pre-fix tree. It is the failing baseline this packet requires and
nobody advanced it after the fix landed. Its evidence cells are also not blank: they carry the
literal word *"blank"* pointing at a provenance table, which is why a grep for adjacent pipes scored
this phase — the one with a real gap — at zero, while flagging five phases that had none.

### 7.2 `001` — 0% against a decision taken

`001`'s continuity reads `completion_pct: 0`, while its `recent_action` describes deleting
`src/views/surface.ts` today after nine measurements, keeping `surface-contract.ts`, and confirming
gate 13 green, vitest 434 passing and verify-placement 87/88 — all equal to the pre-change baseline.
A phase at 0% does not delete a module. The decision is sound and recorded in that phase's spec §13;
**the percentage is what is wrong.**

Consequence for the parent: `spec.md` still names `openSurface()` as `000`'s deliverable in two
places. It no longer exists.

### 7.3 `001` has two names

The folder is `001-overlay-placement-and-menu-language`. `tools/lane/css-lane.json` records its lane
holds as `001-overlay-width-and-chrome`, and the lane's own outstanding list attributes an unclaimed
stylesheet drift to that second name. One of the two is wrong and the lane cannot be reconciled
against the folder until it is settled.

### 7.4 `019` crosses a written scope exclusion

`spec.md` §2 excludes "output number format" as remaining on the earlier track. Report 7 changed
output number format inside this program. Either the exclusion means the *formula editor's* number
format and the parent spec should say so, or the work was out of scope and belongs elsewhere.
Recorded in `019/spec.md` §7.

### 7.4b "No lane activity" is not evidence of no work

This section graded a phase "not started, accurate" on the evidence that the CSS lane recorded
nothing for it. The lane was telling the truth and the conclusion was still wrong: that phase's
delivery was TypeScript, which the lane cannot see, and its one stylesheet edit rode another phase's
hold, which the lane records under the holder's name.

That is now three phases in this packet whose work was hidden by a lane hold, and one whose work the
lane could not have seen at all. **The lane journal is evidence of stylesheet custody, not of
whether a phase has started.** Establishing that needs `git log -S` against the phase's named
symbols and files, and a read of the tree.

The failure mode is worth naming precisely, because it is the documentary twin of the one this
packet was opened to fix: a method that produces a confident wrong answer is worse than one that
produces no answer, and it is harder to notice because the answer looks like a finding.

### 7.5 The grab band has four recorded heights

| Source | Height |
|---|---|
| `003/spec.md`, the operator decision | 35px |
| Lane entry 47 | "widened to the header at **48px**" |
| The lane's outstanding list | **35px** record sheet, **41px** owned-menu sheet |
| `016`, measured on the shipped build | **32px**, full width at 386 of 390 |

`016` is the only one of the four that measured the shipped build rather than describing an
intention, and it derives 32 from the stylesheet's own arithmetic: `--db-space-6`(16) + 8 + 4 + 4.
The likely sequence is `003` setting 48px, `012` later stopping the band at its own header, and
nobody re-reading the result. **The operator's decision does not depend on which number is right:**
all four clear WCAG 2.5.8's 24px AA target and fall short of 2.5.5's 44px, which is exactly the
trade-off that was accepted. What needs correcting is the record, not the band.

### 7.6 Eight phases say "not started" after shipping

`000`, `001`, `002`, `003`, `004`, `005`, ~~`010`~~ and `013` all carry `completion_pct: 0` with a
`recent_action` of "Phase cut… not started" or similar, while the lane journal records their edits
and the working tree contains them. The continuity blocks were written at phase-cut time and never
advanced. This is the same defect as the old §5 status table, one level down.

*2026-09-02: `010` is struck because it no longer has the defect.* Its `goal.md`, `spec.md` and
`implementation-summary.md` all carry `completion_pct: 91` and a `recent_action` describing measured
work, so the phase named here as unadvanced is advanced. **Only `010` was checked for this
correction.** The other seven are not thereby confirmed: `000`, `001` and `002` are already known to
carry non-zero figures in their `spec.md` continuity blocks, so this paragraph's "all carry
`completion_pct: 0`" needs a phase-by-phase re-read before any of them is struck.

### 7.7 Seven sheet surfaces, or nine

Lane entry 47 and `003/spec.md` both say the single overlay fill covers "all **seven** sheet
surfaces". `016` measured **nine** sheet-capable surfaces, all at the identical fill. Either two were
added since the count was written or two were never counted. Low stakes — every surface measured
matches — but the census number is quoted in three documents and only one of them counted.

### 7.8 `specs/context` was tracked as a self-referencing symlink

*2026-09-05:* Commit `c492cf66` staged `specs/context` as a `120000` symlink pointing at its own
path, because `.gitignore` line 10 read `specs/**/context/` — the trailing slash matches only a
directory, so a convenience symlink slipped past it untracked-but-not-ignored and got `git add`ed.
Pulling that commit into the primary checkout replaced the gitignored vendored directory
`specs/context/obsidian-pm-main/` (and its siblings) with the symlink; `tools/live/reference-mount.ts`
imports from that vendored tree, so `npm run screenshots` could no longer bundle. Fixed by untracking
the symlink (`git rm --cached`) and tightening the ignore to `specs/**/context` (no trailing slash)
plus a bare `specs/context` line, so both the directory and a same-named symlink are caught.


### 7.9 The five family drafts each disagreed with the capture true-up, in four different ways

*2026-09-05:* Phases `051`-`055` were drafted against `047`'s research and the capture index's
written descriptions. `050-anytype-adoption/design-trueup.md` landed **after** the drafting started
and read the sweep pixel by pixel, and `050` ADR-003 rules that where a capture and `047` disagree
the capture is the fact. Reported here rather than resolved silently, because each disagreement was
a different *kind* of error and the kinds are the useful part.

| # | Conflict | Where it was | How it resolved |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Adopting a behaviour that does not exist.** `053` D7 and its ADR-001 adopted Anytype's "dual-mode" filter and sort trigger icons; `052` §7 carried the same phrase | `053` D7, ADR-001, inventory T11/T12, AC-102, C2; `052` spec §7 | **Rejected.** The funnel measures `ink=52, blue=0` on a filtered view *and* an unfiltered one, identical to the pixel, across all 120 catalogue captures, cross-checked against `tools/mock-data/anytype/views-report.json`. The sort glyph's blue is a static two-tone glyph. Adopted instead: the `N applied` count label in the settings panel. ADR-001 amended, still Proposed |
| 2 | **A "today" premise the tree does not have.** `055` quoted `050`'s four original thresholds verbatim, and all four were among the six `design-trueup.md` §4 found unfalsifiable | `055` D3, completion criteria, AC-005/009/010/011, C3/C5/C10 | **All four reds rewritten from the tree.** The restore machinery exists (`database-viewport.ts`), `row-menu.ts` cannot render empty, twelve empty-state reasons ship rather than one, and there is no virtualization in `src/views` at all. `055` D3 now names ADR-004's restatements as the binding thresholds |
| 3 | **Designing from a screen nobody saw.** `053` T1 described a view-tab context menu offering rename / duplicate / remove; `054` A5 described a search-first add-relation picker | `053` inventory T1/T2; `054` spec §5B A5 | **Both marked *design inferred from source code, not seen*.** No right-click on a view tab was ever captured, and the shipped filter panel's entire body is one `+ New filter` row. `050` ADR-003 corollary 1 applies: absence of a capture is not evidence of absence, but a packet may not design from one either |
| 4 | **Line drift.** Four of the five drafts cite `file:line`; spot-checks against HEAD found citations off by 6-500 lines | `052` (13 fixed), `054` (5 fixed), `055` (6 fixed), `053` (1 fixed) | **Corrected in place**, each fix verified at HEAD. `053`'s citations were the most accurate — all seven dead-method line numbers exact — and `055`'s the least, including a `120ms` transition count of 78 that recounts to **42**. Recorded because a wrong line number in an inventory is invisible until someone opens the file, and four of four drafts had them |

**A fifth kind, discovered while landing: the evidence moved again, mid-landing.** `964a0b2a` —
the iOS simulator captures, 118 files across 59 states — landed on `origin/main` while this pass was
committing. It supersedes `design-trueup.md` wherever that document reports a phone surface as
uncaptured, which is often, because its only phone evidence was twenty marketing images. Rather than
land five packets asserting a gap that had just closed, each gained a dated reconciliation block
naming the captures its phone rows should now be trued against. **The pixels stay unread** — this
pass could not open images either — so the blocks name files, they do not claim readings. The
sharpest single change: `design-trueup.md` REQ-013 recorded that **no filter or sort sheet appears
in any of the 151 desktop files**, and there are now four.

**What this says about the fan-out, and it is not "GLM was wrong".** Three of the four kinds are
*timing*: the drafts were written against the best evidence available when they started, and better
evidence landed while they ran. The fourth is line drift, which is a citation-discipline problem no
model solves by trying harder. The lesson is the one `050` D1 already encodes — **the capture read
is a gate, not a step** — and the operational consequence is that a planning fan-out needs a landing
pass that re-reads every draft against whatever landed while it ran. That pass is what
`worktrees/086-land-phases-051-055` was.

### 7.10 The five family true-ups measured the same product, and four numbers disagreed

*2026-09-05:* `051`-`055` each ran T001 in its own worktree, against the same Anytype capture set and
often against the same element. Four numbers came back different. Reported here with the winner, the
reason it won, and where the loser was updated — because a discrepancy left in two documents becomes
two designs.

**The rule applied.** A value with a **stated measurement method** beats one that is quoted; where
both state a method, an independent re-derivation decides; where neither is wrong, the disagreement
is not a disagreement and is recorded as such.

| # | The element | The disagreement | Resolved | Loser updated at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Secondary text `#A3A3A3` on the `#171717` panel | `050` §2 says **7.95:1**; `052` C10 and `051` say **7.11:1** | **7.11:1.** `052` states its method — every ratio recomputed from the sampled RGB rather than quoted — and the WCAG relative-luminance formula returns 7.10 for that pair. `050` quoted. Immaterial to any decision: both clear 4.5:1 | `050/design-trueup.md` §2, with the correction noted in place |
| 2 | The `#232323` fill on the `#171717` panel — hover, preselect **and** expanded trigger | `053` says **1.20:1** at five sites; `050`, `052`, `054` and `055` all say **1.14:1** | **1.14:1.** Both sides state a method (per-pixel sample, WCAG formula), so the re-derivation decides: 1.140. Three packets already agreed | `053/design-trueup.md` ×5, `053/implementation-summary.md`, `053/toolbar-surface-inventory.md`. The **refusal is unchanged** — every reading fails 1.4.11's 3:1 by roughly a factor of three |
| 3 | Anytype's motion exit | `047` §10 and `050` §4 report a **0.1s** exit against a 0.2s enter; `055` reads `notification/common.scss` and `_mixins.scss` and finds enter **and** exit on one **0.2s** | **0.2s.** `055` read the source file; `050` quoted `047` quoting it. Our own **150ms `ease-in` exit stands unchanged** — it was chosen against our own motion band, not against Anytype's number — but the sentence that justified it as "the closest in-band value to what the source reports" is withdrawn | `050/design-trueup.md` §4 Motion |
| 4 | iOS sheet row pitch | `054` measures **48.33pt** (object properties), `051` **50pt**, `055`/`054` **50pt** (value list), `054` **52.33pt** (relation picker), `053` **~53pt** (view switcher) | **Not a disagreement, and it must not be resolved into one.** Five packets measured five different sheets. Each figure is right for its own surface and **no single "iOS row pitch" exists to adopt**. Recorded because generalising one surface's measurement to the product is exactly the error `050` made three times (C2, C7 and the page limit) | Nothing updated; each packet keeps its own figure, and none may quote another's |

**Scrim opacities were checked and do not conflict.** Only `051` measured them — page under a first
sheet at **0.519** of undimmed luminance (≈48% black), a parent sheet under a stacked child at
**0.710** (≈29% black), each by dividing mean band luminance with and without the sheet present. No
other packet produced a number to disagree with, and `051` records them as **`048`'s to own**.

### 7.11 `051`'s parity retarget names three values another owner holds

*2026-09-05 (~18:30):* the operator's *"parity by default"* ruling
(`051/decision-record.md` ADR-007) flips fifteen `051` census rows to the measured Anytype value.
Three of those values are not `051`'s to set. **Reported, not resolved** — `051` records the measured
target in its own documents and changes nothing in the owner's file, which is the same discipline
§7's other rows apply.

| # | The value | Who owns it today | What parity measures | What `051` did |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The `condition panel` role's width, fixed at **440-560px** | `design-system.md` §5 | Anytype's condition surface is **288px** on `#191919`, its operator dropdown **232px**, and the `‹ Sort` / `‹ Properties` sub-pages are **360px** — measured off `anytype-menu-set-filter-select-condition-dark.png` and `anytype-menu-set-view-settings-dark.png` (`051/design-trueup.md` §2a) | Recorded the target in rows 29, 33 and 35; did **not** edit `design-system.md`. The owner decides whether the role's width follows the reference |
| 2 | The condition row's **140/140/120px** column floors | `design-system.md` §5 | Anytype's condition editor is **stacked, not three-column** — a rounded format tile, the property name over an operator control, a `Value` field, and a full-width `Apply` row disabled while empty (`anytype-mobile-sheet-filter-condition-text-dark.png`). Column floors are what a three-column row needs, and the stack does not have columns | Recorded in row 33 that the floors do not survive the shape change. The shape itself is `053`'s to build |
| 3 | The file-suggest surface's **top-anchored** search field | `053-toolbar-and-view-controls` (the row shape) | `anytype-mobile-sheet-search-dark.png` is a full-screen surface with the field docked at the **bottom** above the keyboard, a horizontally scrolling chip row above it, results filling **upward**, and an `×` to clear — no header, no title | Row 21 flips from *"recorded, not taken"* to **taken as the target**, and names the boundary: `051` declares what the surface should be, `053` builds the row |

**Why this is reported rather than absorbed.** `051`'s scope boundary is the shell — what makes a
surface a modal or a sheet, and what chrome it wears. A width in `design-system.md` and a row shape
in `053` are both outside it, and a parity ruling does not widen a packet's file list. What the
ruling does require is that the measured value is written down where the owner can find it, rather
than quietly declined a second time under the heading of scope.

### One owner per shared primitive, so two families cannot both define it

The five family phases divide by surface, not by component, so several components are consumed by
more than one of them. Each has exactly one owner; a consumer references it and never re-specifies
it. Verified against each packet's own documents on 2026-09-05.

| Shared primitive | Owner | Consumers, and where the hand-off is written |
|---|---|---|
| **Confirm** (destructive confirmation dialog) | `051` — ADR-003, promoting `openAndWait` (`modals/confirm-modal.ts:45`) to the family's exported confirm with `044`'s seven elements asserted on it | `053` (the sort-conflict confirm, ADR-003 "on drop") and `055` (`design-trueup.md` "This row **consumes** that primitive; nothing here re-specifies it"). `055` deliberately leaves its own ADR-003 slot unused so a second ruling cannot read as the same one |
| **Menu primitive and the picker family** | `052` — `spec.md` §5: *"No phase owns the menu half"*, and this phase builds it (`owned-menu.ts` + `menu-row.ts` extended once, one picker host) | `053`'s toolbar popovers, `054`'s type picker, `051`'s sub-page pickers. `052`'s scope boundary states the inverse edges: sheet grammar is `044`'s, stacking `048`'s, the properties panel `002`'s |
| **Condition row** (filter/sort rule row and its per-format value control) | `053` — one of its five composed primitives, replacing the two condition-row vocabularies the toolbar carries today | `050` REQ-013's phone rows and `052`'s value pickers. **Stated in `handover.md`'s family table since the five phases landed, and in `053`'s own documents only from 2026-09-05** — an owner named in a handover but not in the owning packet is invisible to the reader who opens the packet, and that reader is every implementation leg |
| **Inline editors** (one per column type, extracted from `CellRenderer.startEdit`) | `054` — ADR-001/ADR-002, `src/views/record-surface/`, method bodies moved unchanged, one editor per leg | `052`'s pickers host their popovers; `051`'s sheets host them on the phone. **Same history as the condition row**: in the handover's table, not in the packet, until 2026-09-05 |
| **Empty state, notice/toast, motion tokens** | `055` — its whole subject | `051`'s shell owns a *centred single-line empty slot* and hands the copy question to `055` (`051/design-trueup.md` C7); every family consumes the motion tokens |
| **Sheet grammar** / **stacking model** | `044` / `048` — unchanged, and named here so the family phases are not read as replacing them | All five |

**Two of the six were owned only in `handover.md`'s family table before this pass**, and both are
the components most often re-implemented per surface. The gap is recorded rather than repaired
silently: a primitive whose owner is named nowhere the implementation leg reads is a primitive every
consumer builds again.

### 7.12 The board has been ruled on twice, in opposite directions

**This is a conflict resolved by the newer instruction, and it is named here rather than settled by
a silent edit** — §7's own rule, and the reason this section exists.

| Source | Says |
|---|---|
| Operator, 2026-09-04 | *"copy their board view 1:1 from Project Manager"* |
| `038-board-kanban-port` | Did exactly that. Shipped in 0.0.16, 0.0.18, 0.0.19 and 0.0.20; T12's in-repo half verified at `c563f08`, fourteen carried-forward elements matched to the pixel |
| §4 rows 37 and 38, 2026-09-05 (morning) | Operator on 0.0.22: *"align closer"* — to Project Manager, for the board **and** the gantt |
| Operator, 2026-09-05 ~22:45 | *"Board UI/UX should almost be 1:1 Anytype"*, *"Same for calendar etc."*, *"Board + calendar to Anytype; gantt stays PM"* |

**Resolved: the newer instruction wins, for the board only.** The board's reference product changes
from Project Manager to Anytype; the gantt's does not. Both halves of the 2026-09-04 ruling were
one sentence, and only one half is superseded — writing that down is the whole point of this entry,
because "the board ruling was reversed" and "the port ruling was reversed" are different claims and
the second one is false.

**What is superseded**, precisely: the board's parity *target*. Not `038`'s shipped code, which
stays on main and is the baseline `056` replaces rather than something to revert; not `038`'s
record, which keeps its history and gains a note pointing at `056` ADR-001; and not the gantt,
whose 1:1 copy `037` verified at `30c4b746` — 60 of 60 `pm-gantt-*` classes, zero divergence — and
which `056` must leave unmoved (its REQ-009 baselines the count and the capture hashes before its
first leg).

**Why the split holds up.** Anytype ships six set layouts and no timeline. A gantt cannot be
retargeted to a reference that does not exist, so the operator's line is not an inconsistency to be
tidied away but the only available answer. The table is a third case and moves with neither: it
stays ours, with Anytype grid patterns adopted piecemeal where the captures show them better —
`050`, `053` and `054` already carry those, and none of them is a whole-view reversal.

**What would settle any residue.** The operator's own side-by-side in the vault, which is what §4
rows 37 and 38 have always been. Row 37 now asks that question against Anytype; row 38 still asks
it against Project Manager. Nothing in this repository closes either.

**Three releases carry a board that is now off-target** — 0.0.16 through 0.0.20 and every release
since. Nothing is rolled back and nothing is hidden; `056` rebuilds forward, and
`056/implementation-summary.md` records the state plainly so a later session does not read the
shipped board as current intent.

---

### 7.13 The board's scrollbar is measured, required, and now ruled away

**Named rather than settled by a silent edit**, per this section's own rule. Three documents in this
program disagree about whether the board should paint a sticky horizontal scrollbar.

| Source | Says |
|---|---|
| `050/design-trueup.md` REQ-003, re-measured in `056/design-trueup.md` A10 | Anytype paints one: **10px tall, y 1199..1208 of a 1217px viewport**, 8px above the bottom, spanning the scroller's content width. Colours declined on a platform ground; **geometry adopted** |
| `056/goal.md`, third completion criterion, and `056` AC-004 | *"The sticky horizontal scrollbar exists on the board. **Today: absent**"* — the packet's clearest red, still unticked |
| `056` ADR-002 | The board adopts every captured value. The **only** grounds for declining are WCAG 1.4.11, WCAG 1.4.3 and the 44px touch floor. *"Taste is not a ground, and neither is 'ours is fine'"* |
| Operator, 2026-09-06 ~10:30 | *"for desktop hide or make the scrollbar invisible"* |

**The instruction is not one of ADR-002's three grounds.** It is not contrast and it is not a touch
target. Read strictly, ADR-002 forbids what the operator has just required.

**Resolved by precedence, and the resolution is written down rather than assumed.** An explicit,
in-the-moment operator instruction outranks a rule this program wrote for itself, so the
instruction binds and `056` ADR-008 records **a third ground for declining a captured value: an
operator ruling**. It is a new ADR rather than an edit to ADR-002's wording because *"the operator
overruled the parity rule once, here"* and *"the parity rule always allowed taste"* are different
claims and only the first is true.

**What is deliberately not done.** AC-004 is not deleted and A10's measurement is not withdrawn.
Hiding a bar is not unmeasuring it, and a superseded criterion that still carries its number is what
lets the ruling be revisited; a deleted one is not. `render-assertions.mjs`'s pin is re-expressed at
the ruling's threshold for the same reason (`056/tasks.md` T016).

**What would settle any residue.** Nothing in this repository. The operator's own read of the
rebuilt board, which is AC-010 and has always been.

---

### 7.14 `057`'s criteria are Met at the value level and failed at the gestalt level

**Both readings are honest and they do not agree**, which is exactly what this section is for.

| Source | Says |
|---|---|
| `057/acceptance-criteria.md`, at the 2026-09-06 landing | **8 of 13** rows Met, each on an independent device-pixel read: the grid's 188.57px column pitch at delta 0.00 across all seven columns, the flat chip's ink pair at 0 px of the three former fills, the 44px phone chip against the measured 20px desktop pitch, 119 unmoved `pm-gantt-*` tokens, eight byte-identical gantt MD5s |
| `057/goal.md`'s completion checklist | **7 of 12** ticked after this pass, each with its pre-fix number recorded |
| `review-ui-calendar-2026-09-06.md`, landed the same day | Parity was read **per element, on the harness's default theme**, never as a whole surface on the operator's `#262626` page. Fifteen gestalt rows, **0 of 15** green: the rule is `#282828` against a `#262626` page (two levels, invisible), the weekend tint falls *darker* than the page where the reference's falls lighter, the week starts Sunday against Monday in **all twenty** captures, a spanning event pushes row 1's first chip to **~98** CSS px below the cell top against **32**, `+N more` is a **288x26** filled band, the grid measures **2031px** in a 2000px screen, and **0 of 40** chips carry an icon |
| Operator, 2026-09-06 ~10:40, on 0.0.29 | *"in general our calendar looks nothing like anytype yet"* |

**Neither side is wrong about what it measured.** The values were measured correctly. The operator
was reading the surface as a whole, and a set of individually matched values does not compose into a
matching product — spacing rhythm, hierarchy, density, what draws the eye first. Nothing in the
acceptance table asks that question, which is why the table can be green while the answer is no.

**Resolved in the operator's favour, and the mechanism matters.** Under D3 only the operator's
confirmation closes anything, so a gestalt judgement outranks a value-level Met by construction
rather than by preference. **No Met row is withdrawn** — un-ticking a true measurement to express a
different judgement would destroy the evidence and answer nothing. `057` opens a new completion
criterion instead, and it was written with **no threshold at all** for the hour the review was
still running, because inventing one would have repeated the mistake that produced the
disagreement. The review then landed and supplied it: the threshold is every one of
`acceptance-criteria.md`'s **G1-G15** rows Met, each re-measured per pixel on a corpus recaptured on
HEAD **and** on a second-theme capture whose `--background-primary` is neither `#1E1E1E` nor
`#FFFFFF` — **no row closes on the theme it was written against**, which is the specific way the
first read went wrong. `tasks.md` T019 is the leg. One row is not the leg's to close: the review's
**P0-2**, a Monday-start default, overturns AC-002's written call that the week's start stays
locale-driven, so it is **Proposed** in `decision-record.md` and G7 stays Unmet until the operator
rules.

**The general lesson, recorded because it will recur.** `056` is one report away from the same
position: it also holds a set of value-level Met rows against a whole-surface parity target. A
packet that can be fully green and still fail the ask needs a gestalt row from the start, not after
the operator supplies one.

### 7.15 Notion refinement against Anytype-parity-by-default

**Two reference products now bind the same surfaces, and they do not draw the same product.**

| Source | Says |
|---|---|
| Operator, 2026-09-05 ~22:45, recorded in §7.12 and `056`/`057` | *"Board UI/UX should almost be 1:1 Anytype"*, *"Same for calendar etc."*, *"Board + calendar to Anytype; gantt stays PM"* |
| `056` ADR-002 | The board adopts **every** captured Anytype value. The only grounds for declining are WCAG 1.4.11, WCAG 1.4.3 and the 44px touch floor — *"Taste is not a ground"* |
| `056` ADR-008, 2026-09-06 ~10:30 (§7.13) | An **operator ruling** is a third ground for declining a captured value |
| Operator, 2026-09-06 ~16:10 | *"Based on notion screenshots add phases to all ui improvement phases to further refine based on notion ui screenshots"* |

**Both instructions are live and neither withdraws the other**, which is what makes this a conflict
rather than a supersession. Anytype is the parity target for the board and the calendar; Notion is
now a refinement source for all eight UI surfaces including those two. On any value where the two
products differ — and they differ on chip ink, on density, on where a menu lives — a Notion finding
and a landed Anytype ruling can both be true readings of their own reference and still ask for
opposite pixels.

**Resolution rule, and it is a rule rather than a case-by-case judgement.** **Notion refinement is
additive.** A `059`-`066` child may add a criterion, a task, an ADR or a measurement. It may not
un-tick a measured row, rewrite a landed ruling, or change a parity target. **Where a Notion finding
contradicts a landed Anytype ruling, the child writes a Proposed ADR** carrying both readings, both
measurements and the surface it applies to — and stops. Only the operator moves it to Accepted.
That keeps the failure mode this program has already paid for twice — a document quietly agreeing
with today and lying about the decision that produced it — out of eight new packets at once.

**Why the rule is stated before the first finding arrives.** §7.14's lesson was that a packet which
can be fully green and still fail the ask needs its gestalt row from the start, not after the
operator supplies one. The same shape applies here: eight loops are running against a second
reference, and the moment to decide what a contradiction means is before any of them reports, not
after the first one has already edited a parent.

---

### 7.16 The rename is scoped to everything and excluded from four things

**"Everything, including CSS class prefixes" and four written exclusions are both live, and a reader
meeting only one of them will get the scope wrong.**

| Source | Says |
|---|---|
| Operator, 2026-09-06 ~18:07 | *"Everything, including CSS class prefixes"* |
| `068` ADR-005 | `specs/` is **not** rewritten. A spec document naming `note-database` reports what was true when it was written |
| `068` ADR-003 | `db_view` — the frontmatter key, **57** references — is user data in the user's own notes and is excluded by name |
| `068/spec.md` §3 | The GitHub repository name stays `obsidian--notion-clone`, and the root `README.md` rewrite belongs to a parallel leg |
| `068` ADR-004 | Five `note-database*` strings **survive on purpose**, permanently |

**This is not a contradiction, it is a scope with edges, and the edges are the interesting part.**
"Everything" means every place the product **names itself**. It does not mean every place the old
string **appears**, because three of those places are not ours: the user's notes (`db_view`, the
code fences), the vault's own configuration (`workspace.json`'s view types), and this program's own
history (`specs/`). Renaming in any of the three would be editing somebody else's record to make our
grep come out clean.

**How the conflict is kept from re-opening.** `acceptance-criteria.md` **AC-002** does not assert
"zero occurrences" — it asserts the count equals the five strings **enumerated in AC-006**. A grep
with a named expected answer cannot be quietly satisfied by deleting an alias, and cannot be
quietly failed by a reader who did not know the aliases were deliberate. **AC-009** asserts the same
thing from the other side: `git diff --stat -- specs` empty, and `rg -n '\bdb_view\b' src tools`
unchanged at 57 before and after.

**Why it is written before the leg runs.** §7.15's lesson, one section up, was that the moment to
decide what a contradiction means is before the work reports, not after it has edited a parent. The
same applies to a sweep that will touch 4,314 lines in one commit: the exclusion list is worth
nothing if it is reconstructed from memory while reviewing the diff.

---

## 8. EXECUTION ORDER — DECLARED, AND AS RUN

**Declared:** `009 → 000 → 004 → 005 → 001 → 002 → 003 → 006 → 008`, with `007` off-path.

**As run:** `000` (partial) → `004` → `005` → `002` → `001` → `003` → `010` → `003` → `013` → `014`
→ `012` → `004` → `005` → `020` → `021`, read from the lane journal's acquire order, with `009`
never gating a handoff and `006` never started.

**Not in this order at all:** `022`, `023`, `025` and `026` have never acquired the lane — no
code yet, for any of them. `024` shipped a real, measured fix without ever acquiring it either:
its edit is TypeScript only, in `src/views/list-renderer.ts`, and `styles.css` is untouched. A
lane-journal reading of "as run" is therefore a partial order, not a full one — it says nothing
about a phase whose work never touches the stylesheet. `027` and `028` postdate this reading
entirely: neither had a folder on disk when this pass gathered its evidence.

The declared order was an argument about circularity: `000` repairs the harness and would otherwise
measure its own work through it, so `009` — the running app — had to come first. **That argument was
never tested, because `009` did not run first.** Phases 010 through 017 were then cut in the order
the operator reported them, which is a different scheduling principle entirely and was never written
down.

Two readings, and this file does not choose: either the declared order was wrong and report-driven
scheduling is what this program actually does, or it was right and the program has been accruing the
exact risk it was designed to avoid. **What settles it:** whether `016`'s live drag probe finds a
defect the harness could have caught. That is the circularity argument's test case, arriving by
accident.

---

**Report 1 closed, the drag working on device:** phase Now, target: the operator drags a sheet down
after editing a field. Status: **Mechanism fixed and measured; awaiting the device.** Evidence:
`016/acceptance-criteria.md` — 60px drag moves 60.0px both fresh and after a re-render, against
0.0px after a re-render before. This is the program's oldest defect and the only one re-reported
three times; the check that would have caught it did not exist until now.

**One green gate:** phase Next, target: after the next recapture. Status: Blocked. Evidence: the gate
exits 1 at 12/13 on `screenshots-fresh`; `014` released the lane without recapturing and `004` holds
it now.

**Every orphan owned:** phase Now, target: complete. Status: **Done** — `018` and `019` created
today. Evidence: §6.

**Two operator decisions on the sheet:** phase Now. Status: Open. Evidence: `016` §2 — the phone row
label measures 13px against a 12/14/16/18/20/24 scale, and the record sheet closes outright on a
window resize, which is how one of the two software-keyboard signals arrives.

**A known-inert declaration block left unfixed on purpose:** phase Later. Status: Deferred with a
reason. Evidence: `016` §2 — `placeSheet` writes five camelCase declarations that Obsidian's
`setCssProps` discards, and correcting the names would activate `overscroll-behavior: contain` for
the first time on every sheet, which needs a recapture.

**Thirty-two operator confirmations:** phase Later, target: the operator installs the current
release and reports per surface. Status: **1 of 32**, and that one is an accepted shortfall, plus a
partial on row 29. Evidence: §4. *2026-09-02: this milestone read 1 of 24 against 1.3.3. The
denominator is the row count in §4 and the build is now 1.4.1.*

**Program closed:** phase Later. Status: Blocked on all of the above plus `008`'s release gate.
Evidence: `spec.md` §7.

## 10. DEPENDENCIES

**The `styles.css` lane:** needed by every phase that paints. Owner: `004-checkbox-ownership`,
acquired 2026-08-30T07:57:53Z. Status: Blocked for everyone else. Risk: it is 19,261 lines, must not
be split, and all 196+ captures fingerprint it. Mitigation: `tools/lane/check-lane.mjs` makes the
lane a check rather than an agreement, and it caught an unclaimed drift on 2026-08-30 (lane entry
42).

**A recapture:** needed by the gate. Owner: whoever holds the lane next. Status: Open. Risk: two
phases owe one and each is waiting for the other's edits to settle. Mitigation: one recapture covers
both, which is why the debt was deferred deliberately rather than forgotten.

**The operator:** needed by every milestone in §9. Status: Open. Risk: fourteen reports are shipped
and unconfirmed, so a regression in any of them would be found by the operator rather than by a
check. Mitigation: 1.3.3 is built and installed.

**Two open operator decisions:** `006`'s open target (side panel, full-page modal, or both behind a
setting) and `017`'s status-bar announcement. Status: Open. Neither blocks other work.

**Four concurrent agents:** owner: the operator. Status: Active. Risk: `004`, `005`, `016` and `017`
are all mid-flight, and §7.1 exists because two of them measured the same thing differently.
Mitigation: the lane, plus reading a phase's state rather than inheriting it.
<!-- /ANCHOR:dependencies -->

---

## 11. THE SERIALIZED CSS LANE

`styles.css` is 19,261 lines, must not be split, and every capture fingerprints it.

**Exactly one phase holds the file at a time.** A phase releases the lane only after a full
recapture **and a human looking at the changed PNGs** — `screenshots:verify` proves a capture was
regenerated, never that it looks right. `008` then re-runs the earlier phases' evidence, because a
later edit can reverse an earlier result with no compiler warning and 87 selectors in this file
already do exactly that.

The lane journal is now the most reliable record in this program. It caught what the continuity
blocks missed, and §4 and §5 are built on it.

---

## 12. THE TRAPS THAT OUTRANK THE REST

**A CI check that asserts the defect.** Written during the previous attempt: it requires a widthless
caller to render wider than 320px, and it runs on every push. Fixing `001`'s width policy turns CI
red. `000` inverts it before `001` starts.

**A check that has never failed has never been tested.** Report 1 is the standing proof: three fixes,
three re-reports, and every check on the drag was a string match against source.

**A rule's scope is not its name.** A touch-floor block decided `display` and put a phone-only
control on the desktop (`018`). A `:not(shared-checkbox)` guard took placement with appearance
(`014`). Both passed every check.

**A derived number written in a comment goes stale silently.** `48 = button 24 + checkbox 16 + gap 8`
was true when written, and nothing recomputed it when both controls grew to 28px.

**A fixture with none of the thing in it measures nothing.** See §7.1.
